Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 7879 ms + 669 ms + 11 ms = 8559 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 2033 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 10592 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 8559 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 8559 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 56594 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1228 ms, comparable BL 4119 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 69265 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 403 ms + 1 ms = 403 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 1 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 404 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 403 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 403 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 791679 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 404 ms, comparable BL 24202 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 821520 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 28670 ms + 661 ms + 11 ms = 29342 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 2484 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 31826 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 29342 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 29342 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 56594 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1746 ms, comparable BL 4119 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 69265 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 445 ms + 1 ms = 446 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 3 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 449 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 446 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 446 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 791679 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 449 ms, comparable BL 24202 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 821520 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 6728 ms + 603 ms + 9 ms = 7340 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 987 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 8327 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 7340 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 7340 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 56505 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 886 ms, comparable BL 4037 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 69265 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 394 ms + 1 ms = 395 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 0 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 395 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 395 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 395 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 791557 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 395 ms, comparable BL 24109 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 821520 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 9459 ms + 0 ms + 10 ms = 9469 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 4161 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 13630 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 9469 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 9469 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 56594 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1347 ms, comparable BL 4119 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 69265 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP asset work sum = sum of unique pack/file fetch work + per-asset extract + per-asset overhead across served-from-pack assets = 0 ms + 0 ms + 0 ms = 0 ms
Excluded duplicated waits: 1 ms. Raw paused sum before excluding waits: 1 ms.

TP effective asset wait = TP asset wait sum + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 0 ms + 0 ms (0 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 0 ms

Comparable BL asset wait sum = baseline fetch sum for rows TP served = 791679 ms
Request window collapses overlapping waits into a union: TP 1 ms, comparable BL 24202 ms.
All observed BL asset wait sum = 821520 ms

These are overlapping column sums, not page-load wall-clock. They can be lower or higher than the top-level duration delta because asset requests run concurrently and because top-level load includes parse/eval/layout/app work outside these asset rows. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these sums only to understand where asset-level time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus file-region fetch wait. miss is the first initializer for a pack, pending means this asset arrived while another request was already initializing the same pack, and hit means metadata/decode was already available at request start.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • SW overhead — total − fetch − extract. Browser-side Service Worker response construction and marker-message overhead. No Playwright/CDP fulfill path is included.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + overhead. The wall-clock time spent producing this asset response.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • outside TP manifest — baseline fetched this URL, but it is outside the comparable manifest-handled URL set for this sampled phase. This is not a TokenPack miss.
  • TP did not request — baseline fetched this comparable manifest URL, but the TokenPack run did not request it in the sampled phase. A large cluster usually means the TP page diverged or failed before reaching that import path; an isolated row can also come from feature flags, lazy imports, version drift, or conditional app flow.
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Legend
  • Baseline — page run with recorded static assets fulfilled from the local isolated asset store. Live document/API requests may still go to origin.
  • Baseline-CDP — same CDP fulfill rig as TP for manifest assets, but bytes come from the recorded local asset store instead of live origin.
  • TokenPack — browser Service Worker serves manifest assets from packed PNGs. No Playwright/CDP per-asset fulfill path is included.
  • Δ TP vs BL (prod fairness) — TP minus baseline. Real comparison including harness handicap. If TP wins here, it wins despite the rig.
  • Δ TP vs BL-CDP (tech fairness) — TP minus baseline-CDP. Both pay harness tax, so this isolates pack-vs-direct. Tech-level win.
  • SW TP measurement — direct Service Worker measurement. The report does not apply the old CDP harness-tax projection in this mode.
